- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Alexander, leader of Greece (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Greece
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Hoplite, Replacing Spearman (Requires technology: Bronze Working)
        - Unique Unit: Klepht, Replacing Commando (Requires technology: Railroad)
        - Unique Building: Gymnasion, Replacing Amphitheater (Requires technology: Drama and Poetry)
        - Unique Building: Parthenon, Replacing Scrivener's Office (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Civilization Ability:  Influence degrades at half and recovers at twice the normal rate. +5% Combat Strength of owned and Allied Units for each City-State alliance (up to +25%). Treat neutral City-State territory as friendly territory.
      - Leader: Alexander
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Peace, Land, Bread (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Peace, Land, Bread: -2 Unhappiness from Poverty and Growth increased by 20% in all Cities. Building maintenance reduced by 20%.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 1
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10

        - DenounceWillingness: 10

        - Chattiness: 3
        - DeceptiveBias: 5
        - Rationale: Maintain maximum aggression towards Ethiopia (cultural victory imminent) and Songhai (near elimination). High city-state competitiveness secures diplomatic backup. High loyalty ensures vassal stability. No changes needed.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Spaceflight Pioneers (Policy)
        - Rationale: Iron Curtain will be adopted next turn. After that, Spaceflight Pioneers (Order Level 3) provides a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist, boosts science, and allows Great Engineers to finish spaceship parts. This synergizes with our high science output, preparing for either science victory backup or using Engineers to hurry critical military projects.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Carthage
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Carthage is our vassal and effectively neutralized. Reset to neutral baseline since war is won (Gades remains but poses no threat). This prevents unnecessary diplomatic penalties and allows peaceful relations with our subjugated opponent.
        - UpdatedTurn: 267
        
        ### Babylon
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Babylon is our vassal and no longer a military threat. Resetting relationship modifiers to neutral ensures our AI treats them appropriately as a subjugated ally, preventing unnecessary diplomatic friction and allowing peaceful management of the vassal relationship.
        - UpdatedTurn: 329
        
        ### Songhai
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Songhai is at 100% war score; we must continue capturing their remaining cities (Gao, Taghaza, etc.) to eliminate them for domination victory. Lock in maximum hostility to prevent any peace and ensure total conquest.
        - UpdatedTurn: 375
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Japan is allied with Ethiopia and has defensive pacts with Mongolia and Ottomans. They may join the war against us. Maximum hostility prepares our AI for potential conflict and ensures no friendly diplomatic actions.
        - UpdatedTurn: 375
        
        ### Mongolia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Mongolia is allied with Ethiopia and has defensive pacts with Japan and Ottomans. They remain a military threat. Maximum hostility ensures our AI treats them as an enemy and prepares for possible war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 375
        
        ### The Ottomans
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Ottomans have defensive pacts with Japan and Mongolia and are allied with Ethiopia. They are likely to join any conflict. Maximum hostility ensures our AI is ready for naval and land defenses and treats them as an immediate threat.
        - UpdatedTurn: 375
        
        ### Ethiopia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Ethiopia has achieved a cultural victory condition (7/7 influential). Our only path to prevent their ultimate victory is total conquest. Maximum hostility ensures no peace deals are considered and our AI relentlessly attacks Ethiopian cities until they are eliminated. We must capture Harar, Gondar, Axum, and their capital Addis Ababa to destroy their tourism base.
        - UpdatedTurn: 390
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Plastics completes in 1 turn, then Atomic Theory unlocks nuclear weapons. Increasing Nuke and UseNuke flavors prepares the AI to prioritize Manhattan Project and nuclear arsenal for rapid city capture against heavily defended Ethiopian and Songhai cities, and for deterrence against the Mongolia-Japan-Ottomans bloc. All other military flavors remain maxed to sustain multi-front war pressure.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

          - World Religion: Eastern Orthodoxy:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: 330
          - Mongolian Sphere of Influence: Antwerp:

              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 360
          - Sanction: Babylon:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 330
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: United Nations:
            - Description: Begins the United Nations project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : United Nations wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World. Also, when you expend a Great Person, earn 25 Influence with all City-States you have met. Triggers a Golden Age, as well as the United Nations Special Session of the World Congress if the world has reached the Atomic Era.)
              : Receive an immediate boost of 25 Influence with all City-States. The Consulate National Wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.)
              : Receive a free Great Diplomat.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - Proposer: Greece
          - Enact: Decolonization:
            - Description: Enforce global decolonization. If successful, the Influence of all Players with all City-States is lowered to 50, unless they are permanent allies.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: Mongolia
          - Enact: Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - Proposer: Ethiopia

        You, Alexander (leader of Greece, Player 6), are making strategic decisions after turn 401.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Alexander, leader of Greece (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Greece
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Hoplite, Replacing Spearman (Requires technology: Bronze Working)
        - Unique Unit: Klepht, Replacing Commando (Requires technology: Railroad)
        - Unique Building: Gymnasion, Replacing Amphitheater (Requires technology: Drama and Poetry)
        - Unique Building: Parthenon, Replacing Scrivener's Office (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Civilization Ability:  Influence degrades at half and recovers at twice the normal rate. +5% Combat Strength of owned and Allied Units for each City-State alliance (up to +25%). Treat neutral City-State territory as friendly territory.
      - Leader: Alexander
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Peace, Land, Bread (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Peace, Land, Bread: -2 Unhappiness from Poverty and Growth increased by 20% in all Cities. Building maintenance reduced by 20%.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Iron Curtain
        - Rationale: Switching from Spaceflight Pioneers to Iron Curtain. Free courthouses on annexation and +200% internal trade route yields are vastly superior for a conquest empire integrating dozens of occupied cities. This accelerates economic absorption and lets me sustain a multi-front war while pushing for the remaining capitals.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:

        - Rationale: Mobile Tactics unlocks Mechanized Infantry and opens the path to Stealth, which yields Stealth Bombers. These are powerful, resource-independent units ideal for the late-game conquest rush needed to seize Addis Ababa, Kyoto, Karakorum, and Istanbul before Ethiopia completes its cultural victory.
    - #2:

        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Resetting relations with Songhai to neutral. Greece already holds Songhai's original capital (Gao), so this war no longer serves the domination objective. With war weariness at 97%, making peace will free forces and reduce happiness strain for the crucial Ethiopian campaign.
    - #3:

Happiness and production are elevated to stabilize the empire amid high war weariness.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Game State Overview:**
            - I'm playing as Greece (Alexander) in the Atomic Era (Turn 401)
            - I'm pursuing a Domination victory, currently controlling 4 out of 8 capitals needed (50%)
            - Current wars: Songhai (100% war score, high war weariness 97%), Ethiopia (16% war score, 44% weariness), Mongolia (6% war score, 3% weariness)
            - I have two vassals: Babylon and Carthage
            - My military strength: 7352
            - Science per turn: 2735, researching Atomic Theory (8 turns left)
            
            **Key Observations:**
            
            1. **Diplomatic Situation:**
               - Greece is sanctioned by the World Congress
               - World Congress voting in 3 turns
               - I proposed United Nations
               - Ethiopia proposed Passport System (tourism boost)
               - Mongolia proposed Decolonization
            
            2. **Victory Progress:**
               - Domination: Need 4 more capitals. Current capitals controlled: Gao, Babylon, Carthage, Athens
               - Available capitals to take: Songhai (Gao already taken), Ethiopia (Addis Ababa), Japan (Kyoto), Mongolia (Karakorum), Ottomans (Istanbul)
               - Note: Gao is listed as controlled but that's Songhai's former capital. Wait, Gao IS one of my cities now. So I have Athens, Babylon (Babylon), Carthage (Carthage), and Gao (Songhai's original capital).
            
            3. **Military Situation:**
               - War with Songhai is basically won (100% score) but high war weariness (97%)
               - War with Ethiopia ongoing (16% score, 44% weariness) 
               - War with Mongolia just started (6% score, 3% weariness)
               - Ottomans have massive military (13286) but are unhappy (48% happiness, combat penalty)
               - Japan has military of 6050
               - Ethiopia has military of 1321
               - Songhai has military of 1298
            
            4. **Cultural Victory Check:**
               - Ethiopia already has 7/7 influential civs and 100% policies. They just need to build Citizen Earth Protocol. This is URGENT - Ethiopia is about to win Cultural victory!
               - Wait, let me re-read. "InfluentialCivs: 7" and "PolicyPercentage: 100%". Yes, Ethiopia is extremely close to cultural victory. They need two Tier 3 tenets and the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
            
            5. **My Cities:**
               - 16 cities, most producing "Infinite Project: Research"
               - Several cities building Broadcast Towers (good for culture/tourism defense)
               - Some cities have happiness issues (-3, -8, -9, -4)
            
            6. **Current Flavors/Strategy:**
               - GrandStrategy: Conquest
               - Very military-heavy flavors (Offense: 100, Defense: 100, etc.)
               - Policy next: Spaceflight Pioneers (but I need to check if this is still appropriate)
            
            **Critical Analysis:**
            
            The most pressing issue is that **Ethiopia is about to win a Cultural Victory**. They have influenced all 7 civilizations and have 100% policies. They likely already have an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets (Order, with 3 policies adopted). The only thing they may need is the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder. I need to stop them immediately.
            
            My options:
            1. Keep current Conquest strategy and focus on taking Ethiopian cities/capital
            2. Shift to Spaceship/Science temporarily as a defensive measure
            3. Push hard for domination by conquering more capitals quickly
            
            Wait, I should check whether Ethiopia already has the wonder or if they're building it. The report says they have PolicyPercentage 100 and InfluentialCivs 7. For Cultural victory in Vox Populi, they need:
            - Influence all civs (done: 7/7)
            - Get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets (they have Order: 3, so they have at least some tenets... need to check if they have 2 Tier 3)
            - Finish Citizen Earth Protocol wonder
            
            Looking at Ethiopia's policies: Order: 3. That means they've adopted Order but only have 3 tenets. They need to finish the Order tree (all 6? or get 2 Tier 3?). Wait, in VP Order has: Level 1 x4, Level 2 x2, Level 3 x3. They have 3 policies in Order, which could be all Level 1. They may not yet have two Tier 3 tenets. But they are close.
            
            Actually, looking at the victory progress section: "Cultural Victory: CivsNeeded: 7, Contender: Ethiopia". This means Ethiopia is actively contending and likely very close.
            
            However, my current wars:
            - Songhai: Almost done, but 97% war weariness means my people are exhausted.
            - Ethiopia: 16% war score, 44% weariness
            - Mongolia: 6% war score, 3% weariness
            
            Taking Addis Ababa (Ethiopia's capital) would:
            - Give me another capital toward domination
            - Potentially slow down Ethiopia's cultural victory
            
            But there's a problem: I'm already at war with Ethiopia AND my war weariness is 44%. Taking their capital might be difficult since they have decent defenses and I need to push into their territory.
            
            Wait, look at the tactical zones around Ethiopia:
            - Zone 1064 (Addis Ababa): Even dominance, friendly strength 1697 vs enemy 1231
            - Zone 1187 (Harar): Dominance Friendly, Steamroll posture! Friendly 3249 vs enemy 790
            - Zone 7902 (Bahir Dar): Enemy dominance, withdraw posture. Enemy 992 vs friendly 36
            - Zone 8536 (Adama): Enemy dominance, enemy strength 1336
            - Zone 8573 (Mek'ele): Enemy dominance, enemy 1039
            
            So I have mixed results in Ethiopia. Harar is being steamrolled, but Bahir Dar/Adama/Mek'ele are enemy-dominated. Addis Ababa is even.
            
            Actually, looking at my war with Songhai: 100% war score, 97% war weariness. This means I can probably make peace and take some cities or just end it. But Songhai's original capital (Gao) is already mine. Do they have another city that was once a capital? No, Gao was their original capital. So I don't need to stay at war with Songhai for domination purposes.
            
            For Mongolia: Their capital Karakorum is at (50,41). We're at war with 6% score. But Mongolia is far from me, and they have decent military (5506). Also they're allied with Japan and Ottomans via defensive pacts.
            
            For Ottomans: Huge military (13286) but unhappy. Not at war with me currently.
            
            For Japan: Military 6050, at war with Ethiopia but not me.
            
            **Strategy decisions:**
            
            1. **Grand Strategy:** I should probably stay Conquest to win domination, but I need to be smart about it. However, Ethiopia's cultural victory threat is real. Maybe I should consider switching to a defensive posture against Ethiopia while pursuing other capitals?
            
            Actually, wait - to stop a cultural victory, I can either:
            - Conquer their cities (reducing their culture/tourism output)
            - Get more culture myself to resist influence
            - Win domination before they finish
            
            Since I already have 4/8 capitals, if I can get 4 more relatively quickly, I win Domination. The remaining original capitals I don't control are:
            - Addis Ababa (Ethiopia) - at war, close
            - Kyoto (Japan) - far, not at war
            - Karakorum (Mongolia) - at war, far
            - Istanbul (Ottomans) - very strong, not at war
            
            Plus maybe others I missed? Let's check: Player list has Songhai, Babylon, Ottomans, Japan, Carthage, Mongolia, Ethiopia, Greece = 8 major civs. I control Athens (mine), Babylon (vassal?), Carthage (vassal?), Gao (Songhai's). Wait, vassals still count their capital as controlled by me for domination? The victory screen says "CapitalsControlled: Gao, Babylon, Carthage, Athens". So vassal capitals count!
            
            But wait, if Babylon and Carthage are my vassals, their capitals count as controlled. So I have 4. I need 4 more: Addis Ababa, Kyoto, Karakorum, Istanbul.
            
            All four are available. But Istanbul is owned by Ottomans with massive military. Kyoto is owned by Japan (military 6050). Karakorum is Mongolia (5500 military). Addis Ababa is Ethiopia (1321 military).
            
            The easiest path is Ethiopia. If I take Addis Ababa, that's 5/8. Then I'd need 3 more. Japan and Mongolia are far. Ottomans are strong but unhappy.
            
            Actually, can I continue to conquer while Ethiopia builds their wonder? The Cultural victory requires them to build Citizen Earth Protocol. If I take their capital or severely damage their empire, they may not be able to build it. But more importantly, I need to win before they do.
            
            Given my current military position:
            - Harar zone is being steamrolled. I should push this advantage to take Harar, then move on Addis Ababa.
            - Songhai war is essentially won. Peace would reduce war weariness.
            
            But there's the Ottomans... they have 13286 military strength and are unhappy. They might declare war on me. However, they are currently at war with Songhai.
            
            Wait, I should look at my relationships again:
            - Ottomans: Public -100, Private -100 (I set them to -100). But they aren't at war with me? The report says "Distance: Close" with no war status. Wait, under Relationships for Ottomans it just says "Distance: Close". No war! Even though I have -100 relations, we're not at war.
            
            Actually, let me check Players section for Ottomans: "Relationships: Greece: Distance: Close". No war! So despite -100 relations, we're at peace. That's interesting. Same with Japan: "Distance: Far, Denounced Them" - not at war.
            
            So my active wars are: Songhai, Ethiopia, Mongolia.
            
            Wait, but I also see "WarBias: 10" and very aggressive persona. The in-game AI might declare more wars.
            
            Let me think about the most efficient path to victory.
            
            **Option A: Domination Rush**
            Stay Conquest, finish Ethiopia (take Addis Ababa), then pivot to either Japan/Mongolia/Ottomans. But Japan and Mongolia are allied with defensive pacts. Ottomans are huge and strong but unhappy.
            
            **Option B: Science pivot**
            Switch to Spaceship, get nukes (Atomic Theory leads to Nuclear Fission/etc), use nukes to break enemy capitals. But this takes time.
            
            **Option C: Cultural defense**
            Boost my own culture to prevent Ethiopia from influencing me? Too late, they already have 102% on me.
            
            **Option D: United Nations / Diplomatic**
            Not viable, I have 21 votes, need 45.
            
            Actually, the fastest and most reliable path is Domination. Taking Addis Ababa is feasible. Then assess. But Ethiopia's cultural victory... if they build Citizen Earth Protocol, they win. Can I capture their capital before they build it? They have 10 cities and decent production.
            
            Looking at Ethiopia's cities: Addis Ababa (pop 34, many wonders), Harar (pop 20), Adwa (pop 23), Lalibela (pop 23), Gondar (pop 23), Axum (pop 23), Dire Dawa (pop 19), Bahir Dar (pop 19), Adama (pop 19), Mek'ele (pop 18). Their capital has lots of wonders but I don't see Citizen Earth Protocol listed. They might be building it now or soon.
            
            Given the urgency, I should:
            1. **Set flavors to prioritize offensive operations against Ethiopia** - but wait, my military is already heavily offense-oriented.
            2. **Make peace with Songhai** if possible to reduce war weariness, or just keep pushing since it's almost done.
            3. **Push hard for Addis Ababa**.
            
            Wait, the game just processed turn 401. My war weariness with Songhai is 97% - that's extremely high. I should probably try to make peace with Songhai to reduce unhappiness/war weariness, unless I can capture their remaining cities quickly.
            
            But for domination I only need capitals, and I already have Gao. Songhai has no other original capitals. So staying at war with Songhai just gives me weariness with no victory benefit. **I should push for peace with Songhai.**
            
            Similarly, Mongolia: Karakorum is far away. Is it worth staying at war? Mongolia has 5506 military. The war score is only 6%. Maybe I should make peace and focus on Ethiopia.
            
            Ethiopia is the priority target. They have the weakest military (1321) and their capital is one I need. Also, stopping their cultural victory is critical.
            
            Actually, I can't directly make peace through tools - the in-game AI handles that based on diplomatic relationships. But I can set relationships and flavors to encourage it.
            
            Wait, looking at my persona:
            - WarBias: 10 (very high)
            - Boldness: 10
            - Meanness: 10
            
            This makes the AI very aggressive and unlikely to accept peace. But with 97% war weariness against Songhai, my cities might be suffering. I should consider reducing WarBias temporarily to allow peace, or keep it if I think I can push through.
            
            Actually, I notice my cities have varying happiness levels but overall "HappinessSituation: Happy" at 55%. So overall empire is okay, but some occupied cities have negative happiness.
            
            Let me reconsider the policy choice. Current next policy: Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Order tenet). But wait, I have Order with 5 tenets adopted:
            - Patriotic War
            - Double Agents
            - Dictatorship of the Proletariat
            - Workers' Faculties
            - Five-Year Plan
            
            So I need one more for Level 3? Actually I have 5 tenets. Let me check: in VP, Order tree has 4 level-1, 2 level-2, 3 level-3. The ones I have:
            - Patriotic War = Level 2?
            - Double Agents = Level 2?
            - Dictatorship of the Proletariat = Level 2?
            - Workers' Faculties = Level 1?
            - Five-Year Plan = Level 1?
            
            Hmm, I don't know the exact levels. But Spaceflight Pioneers is a Level 3 tenet. I don't know if I'm eligible. The game says next policy is Spaceflight Pioneers, so I guess I am.
            
            But is Spaceflight Pioneers the right choice? It gives Great Engineer and Great Scientist, and allows finishing spaceship parts with GE. This aligns with a science victory. But I'm going for domination. 
            
            Order tenets available:
            - Spaceflight Pioneers (L3): Science/Space victory focused
            - Socialist Realism (L3): Tourism/Production  
            - Iron Curtain (L3): Internal trade routes, courthouses, city connections
            
            For domination, Iron Curtain is amazing: Free Courthouse upon annexation, +200% food/production from internal trade routes. Since I'm conquering many cities, this would save me from building courthouses and boost my economy. This is much better than Spaceflight Pioneers for my current strategy.
            
            Wait, but my current policy is set to Spaceflight Pioneers. Should I change it to Iron Curtain? Yes, absolutely. Iron Curtain is one of the best domination tenets in the game.
            
            Actually, looking at the options, the policies listed under "Policies" are available options. I can set Iron Curtain as my next policy. But I should also check if I have enough policies adopted in Order to take a Level 3 tenet. Typically you need to adopt a certain number before unlocking Level 3. Since I already have 5 Order policies and the game is allowing Spaceflight Pioneers, it should allow Iron Curtain too.
            
            Let me double check: The policy options under "Options" include the Order tenets. I can choose any of them. 
            
            Actually, wait - my current research is Atomic Theory (8 turns left). After that, I could go for:
            - Electronics (leads to Computers, Robotics, etc.)
            - Nuclear Fission (leads to nukes, satellites)
            - Penicillin (leads to Electronics)
            - Mobile Tactics (Mechanized Infantry, Stealth)
            
            For domination, Nuclear Fission gives me Atomic Bombs. But I need Manhattan Project and Uranium. I have 0 Uranium listed in my resources! Actually, looking at my resources: I don't see Uranium listed at all. Ottomans have 3, Japan has 4. If I don't have Uranium, Nuclear Fission is less immediately useful (though I could trade or conquer some).
            
            Electronics leads to Computers, which leads to Robotics, etc. Mobile Tactics gives Mechanized Infantry (no resources needed).
            
            Actually, for domination, I should probably research Mobile Tactics or Penicillin next, or just whatever leads to better units. But I don't see the full tech tree. Let me think about what's most useful.
            
            After Atomic Theory:
            - Nuclear Fission: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics. Nukes (if I have uranium).
            - Refrigeration: Offshore platforms, Electronics.
            - Penicillin: Marines, Medical Lab, Workers get Fallout reduction, Electronics.
            - Mobile Tactics: Mechanized Infantry, Stealth.
            
            Mechanized Infantry is a strong unit that doesn't require resources. Marines are good for amphibious assaults (useful against Japan/Ottomans if needed). Nukes are powerful but I lack Uranium.
            
            I think **Mobile Tactics** is a solid choice for pure military progression without resource dependencies. But actually, I should check if I need any specific tech for my current unit upgrades.
            
            Looking at my units: I have T-34s, Tanks, Light Tanks, Infantry, Machine Guns, etc. Mechanized Infantry is a step up from Infantry (strength 62). Actually Mechanized Infantry is strength 75? No, in base BNW it's 50 before upgrades. Wait, in VP it might be different. Let me check... actually the Unit Stats show:
            - Infantry: Strength 62
            - No Mechanized Infantry listed.
            
            Okay, so I don't know its exact stats. Mobile Tactics unlocking it is still useful. But actually, I should check: in VP late game, the tech path for military might be different. Without seeing full stats, I'll go with what seems useful.
            
            Actually, looking more carefully: I already have Plastics (just researched). Combined with my strong industrial base, I'm in good shape.
            
            For technology, since my grand strategy is conquest, I should pick a military tech. But the AI will choose automatically if I don't set it. I'll set **Mobile Tactics** as next research since it gives Mechanized Infantry and Stealth (Jet fighters?), both useful for continued conquest.
            
            Wait, looking at the tech options again:
            - Refrigeration -> Work Boats Offshore Platform, +Trade Route distance -> Electronics, Computers
            - Nuclear Fission -> Bazooka, Atomic Bombs, Nuclear Plant -> Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
            - Penicillin -> Marine, Medical Lab, Worker Fallout reduction -> Electronics
            - Mobile Tactics -> Mechanized Infantry -> Stealth
            
            Actually Stealth is a late game military tech path. Mobile Tactics leads to Stealth which gives Stealth Bombers. That's very strong!
            
            For conquest, Stealth Bombers are amazing. So Mobile Tactics -> Stealth is a good path.
            
            But I also need to consider: what about the spaceship/science angle as a backup? No, I'm too committed to conquest and Ethiopia is going to win culturally if I don't act fast. I need to stay focused on military.
            
            **Policy Change:**
            Change from Spaceflight Pioneers to **Iron Curtain**. Rationale: Free courthouses on annexation is incredibly powerful for an expanding conquest empire, and the internal trade route/production/gold bonuses help sustain a large empire at war.
            
            **Research Change:**
            Set **Mobile Tactics** as next research. Rationale: Mechanized Infantry provides strong frontline units, and the path to Stealth yields Stealth Bombers for late-game domination.
            
            **Flavors:**
            I need to evaluate if I should keep current flavors or adjust. Current flavors are extremely military-heavy with Conquest grand strategy. Every flavor is maxed or high for military.
            
            Given that:
            - I'm already winning militarily in most theaters
            - Ethiopia needs to be crushed quickly
            - Some cities have happiness issues (-8, -9 in Opis, Borsippa, etc.)
            - War weariness is extremely high with Songhai (97%)
            
            I might want to slightly reduce some military flavors and increase happiness/growth to stabilize the empire, OR double down to finish Ethiopia quickly.
            
            But the instruction says: "Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each."
            
            My current flavors have almost everything military at 100. This is actually not optimal because too many 100s dilute the effect. But for the in-game AI, it might still work.
            
            Actually, for this turn, I think I should:
            - Keep Conquest grand strategy
            - Maintain high offense/defense but maybe adjust a few things
            - Increase Happiness to deal with occupied city penalties
            - Maybe reduce Expansion (already 0) and increase Growth
            
            Wait, looking at my city happiness:
            - Sippar: -3
            - Opis: -8
            - Borsippa: -9
            - Knossos: -4
            - Awasa: -4
            - Gao: -4
            
            Several puppeted/occupied cities have unhappiness. Increasing Happiness flavor would help build happiness buildings.
            
            But actually, these might be from occupation/puppeting without courthouses. Iron Curtain would solve this for future conquests. For existing cities... well, they should have courthouses already (most show Courthouse in important buildings). So the unhappiness is from other needs.
            
            Looking at Opis/Borsippa: they have Courthouse. Unhappiness is from Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, Boredom probably. 
            
            Given that my overall happiness is 55% (Happy), I don't need to panic. But some cities are struggling.
            
            However, the most important thing is winning quickly. Ethiopia's cultural victory is the clock I'm racing against.
            
            So I should maintain or even increase offensive pressure. But let me think about whether "keep-status-quo" or "set-flavors" is better.
            
            Current flavors are:
            - Offense: 100
            - Defense: 100
            - Mobilization: 90
            - CityDefense: 100
            - MilitaryTraining: 100
            - Recon: 50
            - Ranged: 100
            - Mobile: 100
            - Nuke: 50
            - UseNuke: 30
            - Naval: 100
            - NavalRecon: 80
            - NavalGrowth: 0
            - NavalTileImprovement: 0
            - Air: 100
            - AirCarrier: 80
            - Antiair: 100
            - Airlift: 90
            - Expansion: 0
            - Growth: 70
            - TileImprovement: 60
            - Infrastructure: 100
            - Production: 100
            - WaterConnection: 50
            - Gold: 100
            - Science: 100
            - Culture: 50
            - Happiness: 100
            - GreatPeople: 75
            - Wonder: 0
            - Religion: 0
            - Diplomacy: 85
            - Spaceship: 50
            - Espionage: 100
            
            This is extremely bloated with 100s. The AI probably can't prioritize well with everything at 100. But I already set this. Should I adjust?
            
            For the immediate goal of crushing Ethiopia and then preparing for another capital, I should prioritize:
            - Offense: Keep high
            - Mobile: Keep high (for fast movement toward Addis Ababa)
            - Air: Keep high (bombardment)
            - Happiness: Actually, maybe keep moderate
            
            I don't necessarily need Naval at 100 if I'm focusing on Ethiopia (land war). But I might need it later for Japan/Ottomans. However, too many 100s dilute focus.
            
            Let me adjust to have clearer priorities:
            - Offense: 100
            - Mobile: 100
            - Air: 100
            - Ranged: 100
            - Happiness: 80 (help with occupied cities)
            - Production: 80 (sustain military)
            - Science: 70 (keep tech moving)
            - CityDefense: 70
            - Defense: 70
            - Growth: 60
            - Infrastructure: 60
            - Gold: 60
